assistant:
[Thinking] I see the issue. The MediaCard uses `router.push()` to navigate, and the back button uses `router.back()`. The problem is that Next.js doesn't automatically restore scroll position when using `router.push()` and then going back.

There are a few approaches to fix this:

1. **Use `<Link>` component instead of `router.push()`** - The Link component in Next.js handles scroll restoration better with browser's native history.

2. **Configure scroll restoration in next.config.js** - Next.js has experimental scroll restoration feature.

3. **Store scroll position before navigation and restore on return** - Manual scroll position management.

The cleanest solution is to change the MediaCard from using `router.push()` to using the `<Link>` component, which properly integrates with the browser's back/forward navigation and scroll restoration.

    window.addEventListener('scroll', handleScroll, { passive: true })

  useEffect(() => {

      const savedPosition = sessionStorage.getItem('homeScrollPosition')
      if (savedPosition) {
        // Small delay to ensure DOM is fully rendered
        requestAnimationFrame(() => {
          window.scrollTo(0, parseInt(savedPosition, 10))
          scrollRestoredRef.current = true
        })
      }

**Changes made:**

1. **Improved scroll restoration timing:**

   - Use double `requestAnimationFrame` to ensure DOM is fully painted

**Commit:** `0f099d0` - More robust scroll restoration with retry mechanism

**New approach:**
1. **On mount**: Immediately read scroll position from sessionStorage and store in a ref, then clear sessionStorage
2. **After media loads**: Attempt scroll restoration with retry mechanism
3. **Retry logic**: Up to 5 attempts, 100ms apart, verifies scroll actually worked (checks if `scrollY` is close to target)

This should handle timing issues where the DOM isn't ready on the first attempt.

**Deployments:** Triggered for both staging and production.

Please test after deployment (~2 min). If it still doesn't work, let me know and I can add console logging to debug what's happening.

user:
<user_query>
Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
When a user changes filters (sort, type, or search) while the async `restorePages` function is still fetching multiple pages, a race condition occurs. The original restoration continues running with its captured `fetchMedia` closure (using old filter values), while a new fetch starts with the new filter values. Both update the same `media` state, resulting in mixed data from different filter queries. Additionally, `isRestoringRef.current` remains `true` from the old restoration, blocking infinite scroll until the original restoration completes. The async operation lacks an abort mechanism or stale check to cancel the previous restoration when filters change.

Related files (could not be opened):
- src/app/page.tsx (lines 136-169)

</user_query>

assistant:
Fixed the race condition in `src/app/page.tsx`.

What changed:
- Added a `restoreRunIdRef` token and incremented it on every filter change.
- Each `restorePages` loop checks the token before and after each `fetchMedia` call; if filters change, it exits and clears `isRestoringRef`.
- Scroll restoration also checks the token to avoid acting on stale data.
- `isRestoringRef` is reset when normal (non-restore) fetch begins.

This cancels stale restores and prevents mixed data/infinite scroll lockups.

+  const createImage = (url: string): Promise<HTMLImageElement> =>
+    new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
+      const image = new Image()
+      image.onload = () => resolve(image)
+      image.onerror = (error) => reject(error)
+      image.src = url
+    })
+
+  const getCroppedImageFile = async (imageSrc: string, cropArea: Area) => {
+    const image = await createImage(imageSrc)
+    const canvas = document.createElement('canvas')
+    const ctx = canvas.getContext('2d')
+    if (!ctx) return null
+
+    canvas.width = cropArea.width
+    canvas.height = cropArea.height
+    ctx.drawImage(
+      image,
+      cropArea.x,
+      cropArea.y,
+      cropArea.width,
+      cropArea.height,
+      0,
+      0,
+      cropArea.width,
+      cropArea.height
+    )
+
+    return new Promise<File | null>((resolve) => {
+      canvas.toBlob(
+        (blob) => {
+          if (!blob) return resolve(null)
+          resolve(new File([blob], 'cropped-image.jpg', { type: 'image/jpeg' }))
+        },
+        'image/jpeg',
+        0.9
+      )
+    })
+  }
+
+  const getCroppedVideoFrameFile = async (videoSrc: string, cropArea: Area) => {
+    const video = document.createElement('video')
+    video.preload = 'metadata'
+    video.muted = true
+    video.playsInline = true
+
+    await new Promise<void>((resolve, reject) => {
+      video.onloadeddata = () => resolve()
+      video.onerror = () => reject(new Error('Failed to load video'))
+      video.src = videoSrc
+    })
+
+    video.currentTime = Math.min(0.1, video.duration * 0.1)
+
+    await new Promise<void>((resolve) => {
+      video.onseeked = () => resolve()
+    })
+
+    const canvas = document.createElement('canvas')
+    const ctx = canvas.getContext('2d')
+    if (!ctx) return null
+
+    canvas.width = cropArea.width
+    canvas.height = cropArea.height
+    ctx.drawImage(
+      video,
+      cropArea.x,
+      cropArea.y,
+      cropArea.width,
+      cropArea.height,
+      0,
+      0,
+      cropArea.width,
+      cropArea.height
+    )
+
+    return new Promise<File | null>((resolve) => {
+      canvas.toBlob(
+        (blob) => {
+          if (!blob) return resolve(null)
+          resolve(new File([blob], 'cropped-thumbnail.jpg', { type: 'image/jpeg' }))
+        },
+        'image/jpeg',
+        0.9
+      )
+    })
+  }
+
+  const handleCropComplete = (_: Area, croppedAreaPixels: Area) => {
+    setCropAreaPixels(croppedAreaPixels)
+  }
+
+  const handleUseOriginal = () => {
+    setFile(originalFile)
+    setShowCropper(false)
+  }
+
+  const handleUseEdited = async () => {
+    if (!cropAreaPixels || !cropSource) {
+      setShowCropper(false)
+      return
+    }
+
+    if (cropMediaType === 'image') {
+      const croppedFile = await getCroppedImageFile(cropSource, cropAreaPixels)

+                    <label className="flex flex-col gap-1">
+                      Right ({cropInsets.right}px)
+                      <input
+                        type="range"
+                        min={0}
+                        max={Math.max(0, mediaSize.width - minCropSize - cropInsets.left)}
+                        value={cropInsets.right}
+                        onChange={(e) => setCropInsets((prev) => ({ ...prev, right: Number(e.target.value) }))}
+                      />
+                    </label>
+                  </div>
+                )}
*** End Patch

Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
When a user changes filters (sort, type, or search) while the async `restorePages` function is still fetching multiple pages, a race condition occurs. The original restoration continues running with its captured `fetchMedia` closure (using old filter values), while a new fetch starts with the new filter values. Both update the same `media` state, resulting in mixed data from different filter queries. Additionally, `isRestoringRef.current` remains `true` from the old restoration, blocking infinite scroll until the original restoration completes. The async operation lacks an abort mechanism or stale check to cancel the previous restoration when filters change.

Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
When a stale restore operation detects its `runId` no longer matches, it sets `isRestoringRef.current = false`. However, if a newer restore operation has already started and set `isRestoringRef.current = true`, this incorrectly clears the flag for the active operation. The stale operation's early-exit cleanup interferes with the newer operation's state, potentially causing scroll restoration to fail or triggering unintended behavior from code that checks `isRestoringRef.current`.

<user_query>
Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
The `generateVideoThumbnail().then()` callback sets `cropSource` and `showCropper` asynchronously without verifying that the selected file hasn't changed. If a user selects a video, then quickly selects another file before the thumbnail generates, the stale callback will overwrite the new file's state with the old video's thumbnail. The previous code set `cropSource` synchronously, avoiding this race. Consider tracking the current file and checking it in the callback before updating state.

**Understanding early returns**

This means that if we return early when the status is loading, we skip calling the later `useEffect`. When the status is not loading, instead, we reach those hooks, causing a change in the hook count, which leads to error #310. This explains the issue: we've placed a `useEffect` after early returns. Hooks need to remain at the top level and must execute on every render to maintain the correct order.

   const readFileAsDataUrl = (fileToRead: File): Promise<string> =>
     new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
       const reader = new FileReader()
       reader.onload = () => resolve(reader.result as string)
       reader.onerror = () => reject(new Error('Failed to read file'))
       reader.readAsDataURL(fileToRead)
     })

assistant:
Yes.

Right now the crop only affects the **uploaded image** (for images) or the **thumbnail** (for videos). If you want to *remove everything outside the crop* for **videos too**, we’d need to actually **re-encode the video** on the client before uploading (e.g., via `ffmpeg.wasm`), or upload the original and do the crop **server-side**.

Which one do you want?
- **Client-side crop video (upload cropped video file)**: heavier, slower, larger code download, but immediate.
- **Server-side crop video after upload**: faster UI, but needs backend processing/storage and job handling.

Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
When a user changes filters (sort, type, or search) while the async `restorePages` function is still fetching multiple pages, a race condition occurs. The original restoration continues running with its captured `fetchMedia` closure (using old filter values), while a new fetch starts with the new filter values. Both update the same `media` state, resulting in mixed data from different filter queries. Additionally, `isRestoringRef.current` remains `true` from the old restoration, blocking infinite scroll until the original restoration completes. The async operation lacks an abort mechanism or stale check to cancel the previous restoration when filters change.

Related files (could not be opened):
- src/app/page.tsx (lines 136-169)

</user_query>

<user_query>
Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
When a stale restore operation detects its `runId` no longer matches, it sets `isRestoringRef.current = false`. However, if a newer restore operation has already started and set `isRestoringRef.current = true`, this incorrectly clears the flag for the active operation. The stale operation's early-exit cleanup interferes with the newer operation's state, potentially causing scroll restoration to fail or triggering unintended behavior from code that checks `isRestoringRef.current`.

Related files (could not be opened):
- src/app/page.tsx (lines 148-157)

Verify these issues exist and fix them:

Bug 1:
The `crop` position calculation multiplies by 100, treating the result as a percentage, but react-easy-crop's `crop` prop expects pixel offsets where `{ x: 0, y: 0 }` centers the image. The formula `((centerX - mediaSize.width / 2) / (mediaSize.width / 2)) * 100` produces values in a -100 to 100 range instead of pixel values. When users adjust the inset sliders, the crop area will move to incorrect positions, making the inset controls effectively broken.

Server: cursor-browser-extension
The cursor-browser-extension is an MCP server that allows you to navigate the web and interact with the page. Please use this server if the user is asking about questions or tasks related to frontend / webapp development, and you are encouraged to test any of your code changes by using the tools from this MCP server.

Server: cursor-ide-browser
The cursor-ide-browser is an MCP server that allows you to navigate the web and interact with the page. Use this for frontend/webapp development and testing code changes.

CRITICAL - Lock/unlock workflow:
1. browser_lock requires an existing browser tab - you CANNOT lock before browser_navigate
2. Correct order: browser_navigate -> browser_lock -> (interactions) -> browser_unlock
3. If a browser tab already exists (check with browser_tabs list), call browser_lock FIRST before any interactions
4. Only call browser_unlock when completely done with ALL browser operations for this turn

IMPORTANT - Before interacting with any page:
1. Use browser_tabs with action "list" to see open tabs and their URLs
2. Use browser_snapshot to get the page structure and element refs before any interaction (click, type, hover, etc.)

IMPORTANT - Waiting strategy:
When waiting for page changes (navigation, content loading, animations, etc.), prefer short incremental waits (1-3 seconds) with browser_snapshot checks in between rather than a single long wait. For example, instead of waiting 10 seconds, do: wait 2s -> snapshot -> check if ready -> if not, wait 2s more -> snapshot again. This allows you to proceed as soon as the page is ready rather than always waiting the maximum time.

Notes:
- Native dialogs (alert/confirm/prompt) never block automation. By default, confirm() returns true and prompt() returns the default value. To test different responses, call browser_handle_dialog BEFORE the triggering action: use accept: false for "Cancel", or promptText: "value" for custom prompt input.
- Iframe content is not accessible - only elements outside iframes can be interacted with.
- Use browser_type to append text, browser_fill to clear and replace. browser_fill also works on contenteditable elements.
- For nested scroll containers, use browser_scroll with scrollIntoView: true before clicking elements that may be obscured.

### Fix applied: cropped-video upload now keeps audio

I updated `compressVideo()` so the re-encoded (cropped) upload **no longer records “silent” audio** due to `video.volume = 0`.

- **What changed** (`src/lib/mediaCompression.ts`)
  - Audio is now captured via **WebAudio → `MediaStreamDestination`** (no speakers output), then added to the canvas recording stream.
  - After routing, we set `video.muted = false` and `video.volume = 1` so the recorded audio isn’t silently zeroed out by the element volume.
  - Still tries `captureStream()` audio as a supplement/fallback.
  - Also revokes the object URL on completion.

- **Small UX text fix** (`src/app/upload/page.tsx`)
  - The crop modal now says Upload Edit applies crop to the **uploaded video (re-encoded) and thumbnail**.
